How a Foot Injury & ‘Midlife Crisis’ Led to Kings of Leon’s ‘Therapeutic’ New Album ‘O My Beloved’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

"It felt like something that I had buried down and hadn't really addressed," frontman Caleb Followill tells Billboard.

It was bad news when Kings of Leon frontman Caleb Followill broke his foot while playing tag with his children in spring 2025, forcing the group to cancel planned tour dates in the U.S. and Europe. Explore Kings of Leon See latest videos, charts and news

But the incident also provided the first step toward O My Beloved, the Grammy-winning Nashville quartet’s 10th studio album, out Friday (Nov. 6) on the group’s own Love Tap Records — a gentler and more reflective 13-song set that’s the first to be produced by the band itself. Related Queens of the Stone Age Crowned on ARIA Chart With 'Perfecth' Lil Baby Brings 'Fresh' to Life: Stream It Now Lil Baby, JENNIE, Dove Cameron, XG, Boys Noize & More: New Music Friday Guide

“That foot injury was a midlife crisis kind of deal,” says Followill, who formed Kings of Leon with his brother, drummer Nathan Followill, in 1999 (they were joined three years later by brother Jared and cousin Matthew Followill). “At first it was a lot of self-pity and also guilt, ’cause I felt like I was letting everyone down. After a little bit of that, I did grab the guitar and I started to play and the first thing that came out of that ended up being ‘My Whole World,'” O My Beloved‘s lead single.

“It just felt different,” Followill tells Billboard over Zoom. “I didn’t feel like I was writing something that I was necessarily going to put out into the world; it just felt therapeutic, and it felt like something that I had buried down and hadn’t really addressed. So that song came very naturally and very quickly. I just kept writing and the songs that came after that all had that same feeling.”

The tunes were decidedly not in the vein of most of the band’s catalog, including Billboard Hot 100 hits such as “Sex on Fire” and “Use Somebody,” even though Followill notes that “we have always had these moments on each of the albums where we bring it back to our roots, which is more songwriter-driven.” He expected that his new batch of material would be voted down and the troupe would go back to “the Kings of Leon thing which people expect to hear — the rock songs, the fast, up-tempo kind of stuff.”

To his surprise, however, that wasn’t the case.

“I was expecting to be met with a little hesitation, a little, ‘Let’s not try to switch up what people have come to expect,'” Followill recalls. “But it was the opposite. I feel like we’re all at the same place and we all felt like, ‘Yeah, it’s time to let the songs shine.’”

Working in its Neon Leon studio in Nashville, Followill says the group’s approach was “to feel the room that we were recording in.” That included sounds from trains rolling through a station across the street, which seeped into the recordings. “There’s trains all over the album,” he confirms. “You can hear a lot of walls rattling, but I think that adds something. It feels like a reverb or something.” He notes there was no thought of scrubbing that from the tracks.

“It’s not perfect, and we didn’t want it to be,” Followill explains. “We didn’t want bells and whistles. We didn’t want loud pedals and reverb and all the things that can make something sound better. We tried to avoid that. We just wanted the songs to shine.”

The result is an album that’s unlike anything else in Kings of Leon’s catalog. O My Beloved‘s songs are mostly mid-tempo and melody-forward, with an acoustic underpinning and careful nuances enhanced with strings, keyboards and hand percussion. Electric guitars — whether it’s the airy slides in “My Whole World,” the biting solo in “Cold Blue Dawn” or the soaring break in “Feel the Love” — embellish rather than the drive the tracks, making for an ambience that Followill says was wholly intentional in the process.

“Whatever we were playing, we wanted it to feel like a four-piece,” he notes. “At first we thought we were just making demos…but in the process, we would listen back and we were like, ‘I think this is better than demos.'” That, in turn, led to the decision for the band to produce O My Beloved itself.

“We felt like if we brought someone in, we were going to be fighting to make it sound like (it does), and we’re already getting there ourselves,” Followill explains. “We were just in our little paint factory, and the sounds we were getting were so authentic. We got to a place where it was like, ‘We don’t want anyone coming here. We want to do this ourselves. We want that challenge. We want to meet that opportunity.’ It was so freeing to have our own space and work on our own schedule and do things the way we wanted to do it.”

O My Beloved also takes a different lyrical bent — sentimental, philosophical and at times nostalgic, set off by occasional “threads of darkness” that represent the journey taken to the positive outlook that pushes through. At the same time, Kings of Leon end the album with Followill acknowledging “I think we still have a lot we’d like to learn” in “Get You Down.”

“I know we use the word gratitude a lot, but I don’t really know how else to describe it,” Followill says. “I felt like I was just looking back on our accomplishments, and outside of music — at our life and who we’ve become as men and fathers. There was a lot of appreciation and pride for that and the potential to continue doing what we do, because I feel like we’re at a place now where we love it more than we ever have.”

In addition to “My Whole World,” the group has advanced the album with the singles “KOYSAR” and “Cold Blue Dawn,” the latter accompanied by an inventive lyric video created with the Weather Channel using NASA satellite topography to spell out the words. The group will be in London for a show on Nov. 10 and Abu Dhabi on Dec. 3. Australia is on tap for February and Europe for the summer.

A just-announced North American tour begins May 8 in Bristow, Va., and runs into early June, with a second leg during September and October. (See dates below.) And Followill predicts that the approach the band took on O My Beloved will make an impact on the rest of Kings of Leon’s repertoire.

“I feel like we can break our show up,” he says, “and maybe there are stripped-down versions of songs or a stripped-down set within the set. We’re still kinda working on it and playing with some fun (ideas); any chance we get to expand our sound, or even the opposite, those little challenges are exciting to us. We’ve played a thousand concerts; at this point we just want to have fun with it but also give people a great show when they come out.”

Wynonna Judd to Receive ASCAP Golden Note Award: ‘A Singular, Powerhouse Voice’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Previous recipients include Garth Brooks, LeAnn Rimes, Reba McEntire and Alan Jackson.

Wynonna Judd is set to receive the ASCAP Golden Note Award at the ASCAP Nashville Songwriters Celebration on Monday, Nov. 16, in Nashville. The invitation-only celebration recognizes the songwriters and publishers of the past year’s most-performed ASCAP country and Christian songs.

Previous recipients of the ASCAP Golden Note Award, which celebrates extraordinary career milestones achieved by songwriters, composers and artists, include Garth Brooks, Tom Petty, LeAnn Rimes, Lee Ann Womack, Reba, Alan Jackson and Don Williams.

“Wynonna’s legendary career is defined by a singular, powerhouse voice and a rich body of work that has permanently shaped the landscape of American music,” Paul Williams, ASCAP chairman of the board/president, said in a statement. “As we honor her with the ASCAP Golden Note Award, we celebrate a true trailblazer whose soulful, unmistakable sound continues to inspire generations of music creators.”

The Judds — in which Wynonna teamed with her late mother Naomi — received nine CMA Awards: the Horizon Award in 1984, single of the year for “Why Not Me” in 1985 and seven consecutive awards for vocal group (later, duo) of the year, from 1985-91.

The Judds amassed 14 No. 1 hits on Billboard’s Hot Country Songs chart and four No. 1s on Top Country Albums. As a solo artist, Wynonna has also logged multiple No. 1 hits on both of those charts, including four singles and three albums.

The Judds were inducted into the Country Music Hall of Fame in April 2022. In a heartbreaking turn of events, Naomi Judd took her own life the day before the induction ceremony.

Last month, Wynonna released her ninth solo studio album, The Hard Truth, via ANTI- Records. The 10-track project marked her first time co-writing an album. Wynonna is currently on the road on The Hard Truth tour, a 33-date tour across North America.

Joaquin Phoenix Stars in AI Eyewear Campaign Directed by Jonathan Glazer

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Gentle Monster's intelligent eyewear collection launches Nov. 12.

If you purchase an independently reviewed product or service through a link on our website, Variety may receive an affiliate commission.

Joaquin Phoenix is entering the world of wearable AI.

The Oscar-winning actor stars in a new campaign film for Gentle Monster’s upcoming intelligent eyewear collection, directed by “The Zone of Interest” filmmaker Jonathan Glazer. The South Korean eyewear brand unveiled a first-look teaser Friday, ahead of the collection’s Nov. 12 pre-order launch.

The surreal short film follows a salesman, played by Phoenix, who encounters a series of bizarre occurrences inside an unfamiliar hotel. He then comes face-to-face with mysterious figures at the end of a red-lit corridor, eventually discovering that his intelligent eyewear allows him to understand and communicate with them. The full film is scheduled to debut later this month. Related Stories Dan Rather on Trump's First Amendment Assault, Bari Weiss' 'Wreckage' of CBS News and How His Doc 'Rather' Is Now Streaming for Free on YouTube (EXCLUSIVE) YouTube Q3 Ad Revenue Growth Slows, Rising 9% to $11.2 Billion, as Alphabet Misses Earnings Estimates

The collection is part of a larger partnership between Gentle Monster, Google and Samsung, first unveiled at Google I/O in May. Built on Google’s Android XR platform and powered by its Gemini AI assistant, the smart glasses promise to offer hands-free assistance, allowing users to take photos, make calls and translate conversations.

Fashion and technology companies have been trying to make AI-powered wearables the next “big thing” for a while. The wave continued with Meta and Ray-Ban’s second generation of AI glasses endorsed by Kylie Jenner earlier this year. Meanwhile, luxury conglomerate Kering, whose portfolio includes Gucci and Saint Laurent, has partnered with Google to develop its own AI-powered eyewear. In April, Kering CEO Luca de Meo said the company was targeting a 2027 debut for Gucci-branded smart glasses.

Pre-orders for the Gentle Monster Intelligent Eyewear Collection begin Nov. 12, followed by a public preview in New York from Nov. 13 to 23. Registration for the preview will be available through the brand’s website.

Apple TV Renews ‘Knife Edge: Chasing Michelin Stars’ for Season 3

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The high-stakes culinary documentary, which follows chefs around the globe as they attempt accolades from the Michelin Guide, is produced by Gordon Ramsay and hosted by Jesse Burgess.

As the Michelin Guide continues to expand to new cities, so must its television series. Apple TV has given the official green light to season three of its culinary docuseries, Knife Edge: Chasing Michelin Stars.

The series, which comes from executive producer Gordon Ramsay, takes cameras around the globe and into the kitchens of the chefs fighting for the most prestigious accolade in restaurants: a Michelin star. Some chefs are going for their first, some are going for more and many are just trying to hold onto their cred.

Hosted by TopJaw frontman and co-founder Jesse Burgess, the first two eight-episode seasons are streaming on Apple TV now. “I’m absolutely delighted that Apple TV audiences and chefs from all around the world have truly embraced Knife Edge,” Ramsay said in a statement. “Taking viewers on the journey towards Michelin stars is like climbing aboard an epic rollercoaster. You’d better hold on tight, because it’s one hell of a ride.” Related Stories TV Toni Collette to Lead Apple TV Dramedy 'Lenni' TV 'Monarch: Legacy of Monsters' Eyes Season 3 Shakeup -- If It's Renewed

What distinguishes the show, easily Apple TV’s most ambitious entry into the popular food space, is the access its been given by The Michelin Guide. After all, this is an institution that prizes itself on secrecy, deploying its undercover diners to restaurants the world in hopes of getting an accurate reporting of a typical dining experience.

“What I love most about making Knife Edge is that, however much time I spend in restaurants around the world, I’m constantly surprised by the extraordinary people and stories we discover,” said Burgess. “Behind every restaurant is a very personal journey, and for the chefs we follow, a Michelin Star can represent years of sacrifice, obsession and an enormous amount of hard work.”

Knife Edge has been a unique entry into the Apple TV catalog, marrying travel, culinary competition and docudrama. It’s been enough to attain a 100 percent critics score on Rotten Tomatoes and earn a nomination at the BAFT Television Awards.

The series is produced by Apple TV by Studio Ramsay Global, a Fox Entertainment Company. Executive producers are Ramsay, Lisa Edwards, Lorraine Charker-Phillips and Jill Greenwood, with James Callum serving as the series director.

The season two finale dropped on the steamer Friday.

‘Stranger Things’ Prequel Spinoff ‘Other Worlds’ Ordered to Series at Netflix

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The long-gestating “Stranger Things” spinoff has officially been ordered to series at Netflix, Variety has learned.

The show is titled “Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure” and is set to debut in 2028. The announcement comes as part of “Stranger Things” Day, the annual celebration to mark the day Will Byers disappeared in the original show — Nov. 6, 1983.

This is now the fourth project set in the “Stranger Things” universe. In addition to the wildly popular mothership show, which ended its run at the end of 2025, there is also the animated series “Stranger Things: Tales from ’85” and the stage show “Stranger Things: First Shadow,” the latter of which delves more into the backstory of Henry Creel, aka Vecna. The spinoff was first reported to be in the works in 2022. Related Stories YouTube Q3 Ad Revenue Growth Slows, Rising 9% to $11.2 Billion, as Alphabet Misses Earnings Estimates Dan Rather on Trump's First Amendment Assault, Bari Weiss' 'Wreckage' of CBS News and How His Doc 'Rather' Is Now Streaming for Free on YouTube (EXCLUSIVE)

Plot details are scarce for “Other Worlds,” aside from the fact it will be a prequel set in the 1950’s prior to the events of “Stranger Things” and the “The First Shadow.” In an interview tied to the series finale, Matt and Ross Duffer teased that the spinoff would focus on the events prior to Henry Creel being exposed to the otherworldly substance that gave him his powers in “Stranger Things.”

A teaser asset for “Other Worlds” confirmed that, with the video (see below) revealing that the series will take place in 1955 in Marion, Nevada.

“Other Worlds” is co-created by the Duffer brothers and Kate Trefry, a writer on “Stranger Things” and the writer of “The First Shadow.” Trefry will also serve as showrunner and executive producer on the series. Joe Wright will direct the first three episodes and executive produce, with Shawn Levy of 21 Laps, Paul Dichter, Shelley Meals, Andrea Sperling, and Pavlina Hatoupis also executive producing. The series will film in New Mexico.

“We’ve been dreaming of ‘Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure’ for many years now, and to see it become a reality is surreal and exhilarating,” the Duffers said. “We’re so grateful to our incredible team of collaborators: our showrunner Kate Trefry, who has been in the trenches with us since Season 2 and wrote ‘Stranger Things: The First Shadow;’ and our director, Joe Wright, a brilliant artist whose work we’ve long admired. We can’t wait for you to see what they’re cooking up. It’s Stranger Things, but also thrillingly fresh, with a new setting, new heroes and villains, and new… actually, we’re not allowed to say any more. Now, on to casting. In the meantime — watch the skies, nerds!”

The Duffers are executive producing under their Upside Down Pictures banner, with “Other Worlds” serving as a carve out of their current overall deal at Paramount. The Duffers were previously under an overall deal at Netflix, but it was announced in 2025 that they would be moving to Paramount for a deal that covered movies, television and streaming once their Netflix overall expired in April 2026.

“The Duffer Brothers gave me my first paying job in Hollywood, and it has been a joy and a fever dream ever since,” Trefry said. “Now, it is my insane honor and privilege to be bringing this new chapter of ‘Stranger Things’ to life alongside them and our incredible director Joe Wright. With the support of Netflix and 21Laps, we have had the time of our lives returning to all the horror, wonder and mystery of the flagship, while also telling a wholly new story, one we hope will blow your minds and break your hearts all over again. There are so many more mysteries to explore. Welcome to ‘Other Worlds.’”

“’Stranger Things’ has always belonged to the fans, and their passion made it a worldwide phenomenon,” said Jinny Howe, Netflix Head of Scripted Series, US & Canada. “Today, on ‘Stranger Things’ Day, we’re honored to partner again with Matt and Ross Duffer and Kate Trefry to give fans a new dimension of this universe to step into. ‘Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure’ takes us to the 1950s with new heroes and all the heart, mystery, and spectacle of the original series. We’re proud to keep building this world with the Duffers, Kate, Shawn Levy, and Joe Wright, and we can’t wait for fans to see what’s next.”

“All of us at 21Laps are thrilled and privileged to be continuing the storytelling legacy of ‘Stranger Things,” Levy added. “With ‘Other Worlds,’ the Duffer Brothers and Kate Trefry craft a new ensemble and adventure that is imbued with the DNA of our flagship series while exploring a new era, setting and all-new characters, themes and mythology — the seeds of which may have been planted in Hawkins, but now venture into other worlds of spectacle, action and emotion.”

Netflix Orders ‘Stranger Things’ Spinoff Series ‘Other Words’ Set in the 1950s

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The live-action prequel series will have a different showrunner and all-new characters and story.

The streamer has officially ordered a live-action prequel series to its massive sci-fi hit.

Titled Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure, the new series will be set in the 1950s, with production taking place in New Mexico.

Original series creators and showrunners The Duffer Brothers will serve as co-creators, but have otherwise handed over creative duties to longtime Stranger Things writer Kate Trefry, who also penned the Stranger Things: The First Shadow stage play, and Golden Globe-winning director Joe Wright (Atonement), who will helm the first three episodes. Related Stories Business Netflix Taps Mattel's Roberto Stanichi to Lead Consumer Products and Experiences Movies 'La Bola Negra' Ups Planned Theater Count Heading Into Netflix Film's Widest Weekend Yet (Exclusive)

The series will feature an all-new cast and story. Details are being kept under wraps, but the title, 1950s New Mexico setting, and one hint by the Duffers in their statement (“watch the skies”) sure do suggest the show has a UFO/aliens/outer space theme.

“We’ve been dreaming of Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure for many years now, and to see it become a reality is surreal and exhilarating,” said Matt and Ross Duffer in a statement. “We’re so grateful to our incredible team of collaborators: our showrunner Kate Trefry, who has been in the trenches with us since season two and wrote Stranger Things: The First Shadow; and our director, Joe Wright, a brilliant artist whose work we’ve long admired. We can’t wait for you to see what they’re cooking up. It’s Stranger Things, but also thrillingly fresh, with a new setting, new heroes and villains, and new… actually, we’re not allowed to say any more. Now, on to casting. In the meantime — watch the skies, nerds!”

Last year, Duffers signed a deal to officially jump from Netflix to Paramount as part of a four-year deal with the studio which covers film, TV and streaming projects.

“Stranger Things has always belonged to the fans, and their passion made it a worldwide phenomenon,” said Netflix scripted head Jinny Howe. “Today, on Stranger Things Day, we’re honored to partner again with Matt and Ross Duffer and Kate Trefry to give fans a new dimension of this universe to step into. Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure takes us to the 1950s with new heroes and all the heart, mystery, and spectacle of the original series.”

Executive producers include The Duffer Brothers via Upside Down Pictures, Shawn Levy and his 21 Laps company, and Paul Dichter, Shelley Meals, Andrea Sperling and Pavlina Hatoupis.

Stranger Things debuted in 2016 and became Netflix’s biggest show, delivering 1.2 billion views globally during its first four seasons. The show aired its series finale earlier this year.

Amazon will let you text Alexa your shopping list

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The Alexa for Shopping AI assistant can now order groceries and recommend products over text messages.﻿

The Alexa for Shopping AI assistant can now order groceries and recommend products over text messages.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Amazon is adding a new feature to Alexa for Shopping that allows customers to purchase items by texting the AI shopping assistant. Users can describe the products they’re looking to buy and/or provide a photograph of the item, and Alexa for Shopping will search for matching results in Amazon listings, text back with recommendations, and order the item on the users’ behalf.

This launch builds on the existing Alexa for Shopping experience that launched in May, allowing users to place orders during moments where texting is more convenient than using Amazon’s app or website. The contact will appear in your phone as “Amazon Alexa,” and can be used for a wide range of shopping requests, including ordering weekly groceries, asking for gift recommendations, finding similar products to something you’ve snapped a photo of, and more. Alexa says it remembers your personal preferences over time (so if you frequently ask for vegan options, it’ll bear that in mind for product recommendations), and can track deals and prices to text you when an item you’re watching has gone on sale.

The texting capability is rolling out to a small group of Amazon customers in the US starting today, who will receive a text message inviting them to try the experience. It will then roll out to “millions more customers” in the coming weeks, according to Amazon’s announcement.

“Customers often jot down what they need the moment they think of it in their notes, in their messages, in their group chats,” said Rajiv Mehta, vice president of Conversational Shopping at Amazon. “Now with Alexa for Shopping, your personal shopping agent is just a text away. Message it what you need and Alexa goes to work on your behalf — searching, comparing, and buying so you can get on with your day.”

Amazon is one of several companies that have introduced ways to text their chatbots. Last month saw OpenAI launching a feature that allows users to exchange text messages with its personal AI agent, Dot. DoorDash also introduced a text-based ordering feature in September that lets users get dish recommendations and order food by texting the Ask DoorDash AI chatbot.

Protesters storm Microsoft AI data center construction site

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

A Microsoft data center under construction in Amsterdam, the Netherlands, has been overrun by about a hundred protesters, with some carrying placards saying, “Screw your data center.” According to Reuters, the “Stop Dirty Data Centers” coalition is planning actions across 16 countries on November 6 and 7, 2026, including Amsterdam, Dublin, Warsaw, and another Microsoft site in Denmark.

Pure Data Centers, which owns the Amsterdam data center, said, “We respectfully ask those on site to leave safely and engage with us in dialogue about the development and any concerns they may have.” The report also quoted Microsoft saying that it understood the strong feeling that people have and respected their right to peaceful expression but says that it does not condone any unlawful action.

"On 6-7 November 2026 we are taking action across Europe to say Stop Dirty Data Centres," the group's website reads. "Local communities are rising up to stop dirty data centres where they live. We will unite and exercise our democratic rights to speak out, assemble, and protest to hold elected officials to account. We’re calling for an immediate moratorium on large data centres in Europe!" A map on the site shows actions planned at 85 locations across Europe over the coming days.

The group cited a list of concerns about "the explosion of AI data centres across our towns and cities," and claims to be joining to prevent "the irreparable harms data centres pose to the wellbeing of people and planet." They cite local communities, jobs, housing shortages and rising energy bills, the climate crisis, heatwaves and water shortages, big tech undermining democracies, the mental health crisis, increased surveillance and the erosion of privacy, securitization and militarization, and a toxic supply chain as motivations.

Data center opposition is one of the issues that has taken center stage in the U.S. midterm elections, with Americans overwhelmingly voting against them. It seems that resistance isn’t limited to the United States, though, as European countries take measures to limit their unlimited development. For example, Google has been hit with a criminal probe after its Finnish subsidiary, Tuike Finland Oy, allegedly destroyed 816 acres of forest without a permit. Denmark has also hit the brakes on data center grid connection requests as total applications exceed 60GW, more than eight times the country’s current peak power consumption. One Italian council also a 200% tax on data center development in agricultural land to encourage developers to reuse old industrial areas instead.

The Stop Data Center coalition claims that it isn’t against progress or that it wants to close down existing projects. Instead, it wants AI data center construction to be held until governments have established clearer rules on their development so that they do not have leave a negative impact on the environment and the people living around them. “AI, of course, is a super important ​technology and we ⁠need to have our own capacity,” EU tech chief Henna Virkkunen told the publication. But she also added that “it's very important for citizens that they can trust that their energy bills are not skyrocketing and there are not electricity blackouts because of ⁠data centers, ​or they are not destroying the environment with these projects.”

Humana raised its profit outlook as revenue jumped 26%, beating expectations

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

The insurer lifted its 2026 adjusted earnings forecast to at least $9.50 a share from at least $9.00 despite a quarterly adjusted loss

Humana $HUM raised its full-year adjusted earnings guidance to at least $9.50 a share from at least $9.00 on Friday after third-quarter revenue rose 26% to $41.16 billion from $32.65 billion a year earlier, the company said.

Humana also lifted its GAAP earnings guidance for 2026 to at least $15.09 a share from at least $6.52. The GAAP figure includes the noncash reversal of a net put liability of about $1.2 billion tied to CenterWell Senior Primary Care's previous joint ventures with Welsh, Carson, Anderson & Stowe. The adjusted guidance still implies a year-over-year decline from $17.14 in 2025, which the company attributed to the Star Ratings headwind for bonus year 2026, net of mitigation.

Humana filed the quarterly results with the SEC. It reported GAAP earnings of $1.33 a share, down from $1.62 a year earlier, and pretax income of $216 million, up from $134 million. On an adjusted basis, it posted a loss of $0.70 a share, compared with adjusted earnings of $3.24 a share in the year-earlier quarter.

The revenue figure topped the $40.87 billion Wall Street estimate, according to The Wall Street Journal. Analysts had expected an adjusted loss of $1 a share.

The benefit ratio for the insurance segment, which measures medical costs as a share of premiums, was 93.7%, up from 91.1% a year earlier. That was better than management's guidance of slightly above 94%. The consolidated benefit ratio was 93.9%, compared with 91.1%.

Humana attributed the increase to the Star Ratings revenue headwind, higher benefit ratios among new individual Medicare Advantage members and smaller favorable reserve development from prior periods. That development was $67 million in the quarter, compared with $275 million a year earlier.

Revenue growth came from membership gains across Medicare businesses and higher per-member premiums for individual Medicare Advantage and stand-alone prescription drug plans. Individual Medicare Advantage membership reached about 6.46 million at Sept. 30, up from about 5.24 million a year earlier.

"Humana is delivering positive results for our members, our patients and their health," President and CEO Jim Rechtin said in a statement. "We are also increasingly driving financial and operating performance toward being 'ahead' of our plan," Rechtin said.

Medical and pharmacy costs rose no faster than Humana had projected, according to Bloomberg. Humana stock slipped 1.4% in premarket trading on Friday.

GM and Ford warned rising costs could weigh on earnings growth in 2027

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

GM's CFO sees a possible $2 billion cost headwind in 2027, while Ford's 2026 outlook assumes commodity headwinds near $2.5 billion

General Motors $GM and Ford $F Motor flagged rising costs as a risk to earnings growth in 2027 when they reported third-quarter results in October.

GM CFO Paul Jacobson said it is too early to issue an official 2027 forecast. He said inflation-driven expenses could again squeeze the automaker by $2 billion in 2027, matching the pressure it anticipates this year, according to CNBC. Jacobson cited commodity inflation, logistics and memory chips known as DRAM as the pressures.

GM Chair and CEO Mary Barra told investors on Oct. 20 that the company still expects 2027 to be a solid year that improves on 2026. That view assumes demand holds up and that strain from raw materials, shipping and other recent expenses eases, according to CNBC. GM has become more wary that it can sustain the pace of earnings growth it had anticipated. GM released third-quarter results and updated its full-year 2026 guidance that day.

Ford reported third-quarter net income of $2 billion on Oct. 28, down from $2.4 billion a year earlier. It also raised the low end of its full-year adjusted EBIT guidance to between $10.5 billion and $11 billion, from $10 billion to $11 billion.

Ford CFO Sherry House said in a statement that the company is focused on driving efficiencies as it looks ahead to 2027, "enabling us to better perform in the face of rising inflationary costs."

Ford's 2026 guidance assumes commodity headwinds approaching $2.5 billion year over year, the company said. Higher commodity costs weighed on third-quarter EBIT at Ford Blue, which fell 10% to $1.4 billion, and at Ford Pro, which fell 16% to $1.7 billion.

Ford expects its 2027 performance to roughly match 2026, according to CNBC.

Hearts vs Falkirk - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

By Calum Burrowes | 06 Nov 2026 13:56, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 14:02

Meeting for the second time in less than two months, Falkirk travel to the Scottish capital looking to extend their winning run to five matches when they take on an inconsistent Hearts side.

The Jambos were in European action during the week but suffered their second defeat in five days, losing 3-1 to Thun in Switzerland, while the Bairns secured a memorable victory over Celtic to claim their first win against the Glasgow giants in 19 years.

After narrowly missing out on their first Scottish Premiership title since 1960, Hearts entered the summer facing a significant rebuilding task, having come closer than any club outside Celtic and Rangers to winning the championship since 1985.

Their preparations were further complicated when manager Derek McInnes departed to take charge the Gers, bringing several key players with him.

Wouter Vrancken was appointed as the new Jambos head coach in June, becoming the first Belgian to take charge of the club, but he has so far been unable to maintain the momentum built under McInnes.

After 23 matches, the former Sint-Truidense manager has recorded just eight victories, alongside six draws and nine defeats, leaving Hearts third in the Scottish Premiership, out of the Scottish League Cup and 32nd in the Conference League league-phase table.

Hearts travelled to Thun on the back of Sunday's Scottish League Cup semi-final defeat to city rivals Hibernian, meaning Vrancken's men were in need of a positive result to lift the mood around the club.

However, a poor defensive display saw the Jambos suffer a 3-1 defeat against their Swiss opponents, leaving them without a victory in the Conference League after three matches, having also drawn with Nordsjaelland and lost to Trabzonspor in their previous European outings.

As for visiting Falkirk, they make the short journey east in considerably better spirits after securing a memorable victory over league leaders Celtic last weekend, courtesy of first-half goals from Ethan Ross and Scott Tanser.

The victory at the Falkirk Stadium was their first against the Bhoys in almost two decades and continued an impressive run of form following their difficult start to the 2026-27 campaign.

John McGlynn's side have now won four consecutive Scottish Premiership matches, a run that began in mid-October following a defeat to Dundee, and have climbed the table from second-bottom to fourth.

Their impressive turnaround has also been built on improved defensive performances, with only two sides in the division conceding fewer than their 12 goals, while only Rangers have collected more points across the last six Premiership matchweeks.

Bairns' superb run has also earned manager McGlynn the Premiership Manager of the Month award for October, and his side will now look to overcome a Hearts team they have already beaten this season, having secured a 2-1 victory in September.

Despite their inconsistent performances and recurring defensive issues, Hearts have also been hampered by a significant injury list during the opening months of the season.

An injury-hit squad travelled to Switzerland in midweek and returned with further concerns after Stephen Kingsley was forced off shortly after the hour mark against Thun, ruling him out of Sunday's encounter alongside 10 other players.

Harry Milne, Calvin Miller and Blair Spittal are among those currently sidelined, with Milne not expected to return to action until August 2027.

As for the visitors, Falkirk also head into the weekend with a large number of players unavailable through injury.

Despite the joy following their victory over Celtic, Ryan Strain was forced off in the early stages of that match and will play no part this weekend.

He joins Leon McCann, Aodhan Doherty, Joseph Gardner, Ethan Laidlaw, Keelan Adams and Aidan Nesbitt in the treatment room.

Despite arriving in Edinburgh in contrasting form, the two sides are relatively evenly matched in the league table, and we expect Falkirk to provide a stern test for their hosts.

Hearts will be keen to respond after successive defeats, but with McGlynn's men enjoying an excellent run of results, we anticipate a closely contested encounter with the points ultimately being shared.

The One Where The Friends Writers Worried The Show 'Wasn't Funny Enough' (Which Was Bad News For The Cast)

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

Read reviews, interviews and deep dives on new releases.

Get the latest movie, TV and streaming news delivered to your inbox.

Why Netflix’s Matthew Perry Doc Doesn’t Use Voiceover From His Memoir Audiobook, Per The Director

It Was Jennifer Aniston Who Reached Out First At Matthew Perry’s Funeral (But The Entire Friends Cast Showed)

I Just Found Out A Wild Big Bang Theory Scene With Sheldon And Amy Nearly Happened Off Camera

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Friends may have ended 22 years ago, but the series is still on the record as one of the best sitcoms of all time. Between the relatable characters, storylines, and hilarious one-liners, Friends was truly the epitome of comedy in the ‘90s and early ‘00s. Which made it even more surprising when I learned there was a time the writers were worried the show wasn’t funny enough. This was bad news for the cast.

When it comes to a show like Friends, the jokes are the top priority. Friends filmed live in front of a studio audience, and when jokes fell flat, it could take the energy out of the room. This meant the writers and the cast really had to to nail every joke, and every joke needed to be a notch above.

Even prior to this latest interview, I would have wagered a guess this meant rewrites happened on the regular. Lisa Kudrow seemingly confirmed this redo process, opening up about her experience via the Table Manners podcast, which is hosted by singer Jessie Ware and her mom, Lennie. Kudrow confirmed that they did retakes, sometimes on the fly and in front of the audience, and they also did rewrites thanks to one big concern:

It might not be funny enough, right? Or let’s see if we can improve on it. Not us. The writers.

Rewrites are a given for any project, no matter what, but it is crazy to hear the writers of Friends were worried the show wasn’t funny enough. Or at least, some of the scripts weren’t funny enough. The revisions and retakes led to filming longer days than most other sitcoms on the air at the time. Kudrow admitted that on a normal show day, filming would take twice as long as a typical TV series. From what she said, it sounds like it was a slog.

Normally, a half-hour show took around two, two and a half hours, maybe three to shoot. Ours was six or more hours.

Network TV is infamous for long shoot days, anyway, but given the timelines Lisa Kudrow laid out, it's definitely a little insane to think about what the Friends cast went through. And not just them! Kudrow was asked about the long hours and she had to "guess" everyone got overtime. At least the show could seemingly afford it (though it's easy to see how network TV is past the lobster and champagne days now.)

I guess everyone got overtime. The show was doing well. They could afford it, the studio.

That being said, the rewrites may have been a pain for the cast, but I have to assume they helped. Friends still holds up and is iconic to this day. I don't know that is true for those other shows that only filmed for a couple of hours.

Meanwhile, even if the writers didn’t think the show was funny enough at times, Courteney Cox had a feeling Friends would be a success. We know this because Sex and the City star Kristin Davis previously revealed she and Cox were once in the same yoga class, and Cox told her she wanted to buy a Porsche because she just did a pilot that she was bullish on. That pilot was Friends, and, well, the rest is history. Even though NBC nearly canceled Friends before it even aired, I’m really happy Cox was right.

One thing I am curious about? I wish I knew some specific moments that were rewritten, and I'd be curious what the experience was like as a whole for anyone in that studio audience across Friends' 10 seasons. The process may have been long, but it definitely worked, because the series is as funny as ever. Fans can still watch Friends with an HBO Max subscription and see what jokes hold up.

Passionate writer. Obsessed with anything and everything entertainment, specifically movies and television. Can get easily attached to fictional characters.

Bryan opened up about the sweet gesture during last month's rambling, two-hour On the Porch podcast chat with Matthew McConaughey.

Zach Bryan is not one to forget where he came from. The Oklahoma-bred “I Remember Everything” singer and military veteran sat down last month with actor Matthew McConaughey for one of his first public interviews in years to discuss Bryan’s new book and EP, Young Manhood & Other Donatable Organs, as well as the sweet, honorable gesture he makes to his former commanding officer in the Navy who helped get his career off the ground. Related Queens of the Stone Age Crowned on ARIA Chart With 'Perfecth' Lil Baby Brings 'Fresh' to Life: Stream It Now Lil Baby, JENNIE, Dove Cameron, XG, Boys Noize & More: New Music Friday Guide Explore Matthew McConaughey Zach Bryan See latest videos, charts and news

“Is it true you give your commanding officer a cut of royalties on every track since?,” McConaughey asked after Bryan described the crucial role his former Navy commanding officer, Leo Alba, played in helping to produce his 2019 debut album, DeAnn during the wide-ranging, two-hour On the Porch podcast interview with the actor who starred in his 2024 “Nine Ball” video.

“He’s the reason, man. He’s the reason I recorded any songs ever at all!,” said Bryan, who joined the Navy at 17 and served in active duty from 2013 until an honorable discharge in 2021 to pursue his music dream after encouragement from Alba to record a full album while the singer was still enlisted. “And I thought it was justified, man. He’s the reason I have any music at all. He’s the reason we’re all here at this house pretty much. I wouldn’t be who I was without him f–kin’ looking at me and saying I can record those songs.”

Bryan, 30, said Alba’s children call him “uncle” and Alba comes to his shows all the time and, to this day, he gives Alba a percentage of royalties from every song he releases. “Because that’s how much that meant to me,” he said of Alba’s encouragement. “Him believing in me enough to do that for me.”

The interview also features Bryan discussing his sobriety, the difficulty of life on the road, God and the inspiration behind his recently released eight-track Young Manhood & Other Donatable Organs EP, which debuted at No. 1 on Billboard’s Top Country Albums, Top Rock Albums and Americana/Folk Albums charts and No. 2 on the Billboard 200 in its first week of release last month. The EP features a duet with Lana Del Rey on “Lies” and the Turnpike Troubadours on “Plainsman.”

Karin Slaughter's New 5/5 Thriller Series Officially Debuts Next Week

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Lade Omotade is a News and Features Author at Collider, covering everything from casting announcements and streaming trends to behind-the-scenes stories and the cultural impact of today's biggest film and TV franchises. Since joining the team in 2024, she has written more than 1,000 news stories, with her work also cited by outlets like AOL and IMDb.

Karin Slaughter is back with another suspenseful thriller adapted for television, officially debuting next week. Widely recognized for her dark, gripping crime novels that have sold more than 40 million copies worldwide, Slaughter is a #1 New York Times and Sunday Times bestselling author of over 25 books published in 120 countries. One of her most popular thrillers is the Will Trent series, adapted for small screens in 2023, starring Ramón Rodríguez in the titular role, andnow in its 4th season on ABC.

Will Trent isn't the only Slaughter adaptation on TV, as another similarly thrilling one is Pieces of Her. Based on her 2018 standalone novel, Pieces of Her was adapted into an eight-episode TV series. It stars Toni Collette and Bella Heathcote and premiered on March 4, 2022. Upon release, the gripping series debuted at #1 worldwide on Netflix, and between February 27, 2022, and April 3, 2022, it was watched 227.5 million hours globally on the streamer.

Four years later, another Slaughter crime thriller launches on TV as a limited series starring Rose Byrne and Meghann Fahy. Titled The Good Daughter, this twisty mystery series officially premieres on Peacock on November 12, 2026, with all six one-hour episodes exclusively available to stream at once. Slaughter wrote every episode and serves as showrunner, with the iconic Steph Green, Emmy-nominated in 2020 for Best Director for the Watchmen episode "Little Fear of Lightning," directing the adaptation.

Initially, Slaughter wasn't keen on The Good Daughter adaptation when asked to write the series, but she eventually became curious about the screenwriting process. "I chose The Good Daughter because it's always been one of my favorite books," the author told Peacock.

Britain makes more detective television than any country has any business making, and somehow almost all of it is good. Here is the one you belong in, what it says about you, and the case for it being the best of the four.

You want the tea, the gossip and the vicar behaving oddly. Midsomer is a county where the scenery is beautiful, the fete is on Saturday and the murder rate is roughly that of a small war, and absolutely nobody involved thinks this is strange.

You feel other people's grief as if it were handed to you personally. Broadchurch is less a whodunnit than a study of what a small town does to itself when it finds out that the answer was always going to be somebody everybody already knew.

You go through things rather than around them. John Luther is the best detective in London and a liability to everybody who works with him, and the show is completely honest that those two facts are the same fact.

You say the thing and you do not dress it up. Vera Stanhope turns up in a hat and a mac, is rude to almost everybody, and is the only person in the room prepared to ask the question that the family has been waiting years for somebody to ask.

Adapted from Slaughter's 2017 novel, The Good Daughter is a standalone story with a beginning, middle, and end that tells what happened to the Quinn family of Pikeville, Georgia. It sees Byrne and Fahy as estranged sisters, Samantha and Charlotte Quinn, who, as kids, witnessed a traumatic incident that badly impacted their family. Sam was left injured, and she eventually moved far away from home.

Twenty years later, another violent crime shakes the sisters' Georgia hometown, prompting Sam to return home. Charlotte, who follows in her father's footsteps by working in town as a lawyer, is the first witness on the scene. This stirs up traumatic memories of what happened to her and her sister years ago, and they're both forced to confront what happened. Also starring in the miniseries are Brendan Gleeson, Paula Malcomson, Jake McDorman, and Olivia Williams.

The Good Daughter premieres next week on Peacock. Watch the official trailer above.

TikTok faces EU fine over “For You” feed ‘rabbit hole’ effect

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Commission says the app keeps pushing harmful videos; owner ByteDance faces a fine of up to 6%.

The European Commission has preliminarily found that TikTok breaks EU online safety rules by repeatedly recommending similar videos, potentially trapping users in harmful content loops.

The findings, published on Friday, follow a long-running investigation under the Digital Services Act (DSA).

The Commission says TikTok’s For You feed keeps recommending videos similar to those users have already watched or liked. This can happen even when the content deals with sensitive subjects such as loneliness, weight loss or body image.

As a result, users can end up seeing more and more videos on topics that may negatively affect their mental health. The Commission said TikTok had failed to properly assess these risks or take sufficient steps to reduce them.

TikTok already offers some controls, including a button that lets users mark videos as “not interested”. However, the Commission found that these features do little to stop the platform from recommending similar harmful content.

Henna Virkkunen, the Commission’s executive vice-president for tech sovereignty, said TikTok’s recommendation system can lead users “down rabbit holes they cannot easily escape from”.

“TikTok needs to step up its efforts to protect users’ well-being, including those of minors,” Virkkunen said.

The Commission wants TikTok to redesign its For You feed so that it takes users’ preferences into greater account. It also wants the platform to rely less on watch time when deciding which videos to recommend and make its safety controls easier to access and use.

These are preliminary findings, not a final ruling. TikTok will have the opportunity to respond before the Commission makes its decision.

If the Commission confirms the breach, ByteDance, TikTok’s parent company, could face a fine of up to 6% of its global annual turnover.

TikTok did not immediately respond to a request for comment from the Associated Press.

The investigation adds to the company’s regulatory problems in Europe. In February, the Commission also said TikTok’s addictive design, including features such as endless scrolling, violated the DSA. TikTok rejected those findings and said it would challenge them.

The Commission is also investigating similar concerns about Meta’s Facebook and Instagram, as regulators face growing pressure to address the effects of social media on children’s mental health.

Tesla to Wind Down Cybertruck Production as Elon Musk Pivots to Robotaxis and AI

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Tesla to Wind Down Cybertruck Production as Elon Musk Pivots to Robotaxis and AI

Tesla will stop making the Cybertruck at its Texas Gigafactory around the middle of next year, CEO Elon Musk said on Friday, as the company shifts its focus to autonomous vehicles.

"Tesla will be winding down production of the Cybertruck at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of next year to make room for increased Cybercab production," Musk tweeted.

Tesla will be winding down production of the Cybertruck at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of next year to make room for increased Cybercab production

Responding to a user who called the Cybertruck the best vehicle Tesla has ever made, Musk replied: "True, but the Cybertruck fleet will be around for a long time." "Cybertruck was an incredible technology development platform and lives on through Cybercab," Wes Morrill, the truck's lead engineer, wrote in response to Musk's post, thanking the truck's owners and "everyone who contributed to making the future looking like the future."

The stainless-steel pickup, first delivered to customers in late 2023, has struggled to win buyers. Tesla delivered 8,295 vehicles in its "other models" category, which includes the Cybertruck, in the third quarter, down 48% from a year earlier, Electrek reported. That puts the truck's sales at roughly a tenth of the 250,000 units a year that Tesla built its production line to make, according to the outlet.

Musk has made the Cybercab, Tesla's robotaxi, central to his push to turn the carmaker into an AI and robotics company. In April 2025, he said Tesla would launch its first robotaxi service in Austin using Model Y vehicles running its Full Self-Driving software, and begin building Cybercabs in Texas the following year.

Tesla began offering rides in the Cybercab, a two-seater with no steering wheel or pedals that was first unveiled in October 2024, in limited areas of Austin on September 3, Reuters reported. The National Highway Traffic Safety Administration said at the time that it was "evaluating the situation," as federal rules typically require cars to have human controls. Tesla began producing the Cybercab in April, and had 45 of them registered in Texas as of the launch, according to the report.

At the 2024 unveiling, Musk said he expects the Cybercab to cost around $30,000, Auto Express reported.

The shift comes as Musk weighs combining Tesla with SpaceX, which absorbed his AI company xAI earlier this year. Last month, Musk said both companies' boards had formed special committees of independent directors to evaluate a merger, which would reshape his AI empire by bringing rockets, satellites, AI models, robots and cars under one roof. A SpaceX filing has since confirmed that any combination would need sign-off from its "controlling shareholder," Musk.

A merger would also create a Bitcoin treasury behemoth, combining SpaceX's 18,712 BTC and Tesla's 11,509 BTC for a total of 30,221 BTC, worth roughly $2.3 billion at current prices.

Both companies have also blocked their shares from being offered on the tokenized stock venue being built by crypto exchange OKX and NYSE owner ICE.

Penn Entertainment, Wynn see Texas casino opening after midterm upsets

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Legalizing casinos in Texas requires a constitutional amendment and a statewide vote after Dan Patrick lost his lieutenant governor seat

Penn Entertainment and Wynn Resorts said on earnings calls Thursday that Tuesday's midterm results created openings for gambling expansion in Texas, according to CNBC.

Penn CEO Jay Snowden said the company is "ready to play offense" in the state, according to SBC Americas. Penn runs two of the three Class 1 thoroughbred tracks in Texas, with Sam Houston in Houston and Retama Park near San Antonio. Snowden said the company will devote extra attention to Texas and expects to be "positioned as well, if not better, than really anybody in the sector" should lawmakers begin advancing legalization.

Democrat Vikki Goodwin defeated Lt. Gov. Dan Patrick on Tuesday, and Democrat Gina Hinojosa won the governor’s race, ousting Republican Gov. Greg Abbott $ABT, according to CNBC. Patrick has been a persistent opponent of gambling expansion in Texas, CNBC said.

Wynn CEO Craig Billings said on the company's call that "the whole landscape changed" after the election, according to CNBC. Billings described Texas as "kind of a stealth market that is giant," noting that many Texans head north to Oklahoma's tribal casinos to place bets.

The election did not produce a deal on gambling. Legalizing casinos or sports betting in Texas would take a constitutional amendment that clears two-thirds of each legislative chamber and then goes to voters, according to CNBC. Republicans retained control of both chambers of the Legislature. “We are not sure what comes out,” Snowden said.

Ron Reese, a spokesman for Las Vegas Sands, told CNBC the company is not ready to weigh in on what Texas gaming might look like and will keep watching how things unfold.

Penn reported third-quarter results on Thursday, with revenue of $1.84 billion, up from $1.72 billion a year earlier. It posted net income of $32.6 million, compared with a net loss of $865.1 million in the year-earlier quarter.

Wynn reported third-quarter results the same day. Operating revenue fell to $1.69 billion from $1.83 billion, and net income attributable to the company was $17.3 million, compared with $88.3 million a year earlier.

No more Cybertrucks; Musk moves on from the angular metallic flop

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Tesla wanted to unseat the Ford F-150, instead it made the Edsel look successful.

Elon’s Edsel is no more. Last night Tesla CEO Elon Musk revealed on social media that his car company is calling time on its failed attempt to wrest control of the American pickup truck market. The Cybertruck will cease production in the middle of 2027, liberating that space in Tesla’s cavernous factory for other uses—more Cybercabs, most likely.

Musk first revealed the Cybertruck in late 2019, back when Tesla was still trading at a theoretically understandable price-to-earning ratio. The pickup’s unusual angular shape owed more to low-res video game models than any concessions to practicality. At the time Musk made fanciful claims that the truck would use a stainless steel monocoque construction, telling his audience that “the body and bed don’t do anything useful” on a normal truck. While some race cars use carbon-fiber monocoques for strength and lightness, making a pickup truck with a stainless steel monocoque chassis would only make sense if someone had an awful lot of stainless steel lying around and needed something to do with it all.

The truck was supposed to go on sale for less than $40,000, and Tesla made its usual stratospheric sales predictions that (had they been accurate) the bare metal EVs would be as ubiquitous as the Model 3 and Y.

But development of the Cybertruck was more troubled than usual for Tesla, which struggled heavily to bring both the Model 3 and Y into mass production. Musk demanded “Lego-like precision” from the workers assembling the trucks, but achieving the 10-micron tolerances required by the Danish toy company was not evidently successful, based on what we saw when we experienced our first Cybertruck in reality.

That was in 2023, when Cybertruck headlines were mostly about recalls and stop sales. By then, the truck used a conventional unibody chassis—conventional for Tesla, not for full-size pickup trucks. Toward the end of that year, Musk told his audience that “we dug our grave” with the truck, perhaps the first public acknowledgment that annual sales of a quarter of a million Cybertrucks probably wasn’t going to happen after all.

Sales never took off, and the divisive shape of the Cybertruck became an all-too visible reminder of the CEO’s role in reshaping the federal government to his liking. Even selling thousands of Cybertrucks to SpaceX couldn’t save the pickup. Now, with America having vociferously rejected Musk’s vision of governance, those wishing to drive something that reminds everyone around them of that vision will only have a few months more to purchase one.

Nvidia mandates GPU tracking for Southeast Asian cloud partners seeking official status

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Southeast Asia computing providers are now reportedly required to register with Nvidia's Fleet Intelligence tracking system if they want to become official Nvidia Cloud Partners (NCPs). The move, according to Bloomberg, is aimed at giving the chipmaker another way to monitor where its AI accelerators are deployed, as Washington grows increasingly frustrated with restricted hardware finding its way into China through regional smuggling networks. Nvidia was not immediately available for comment.

The NCP designation is worth pursuing as it gives cloud providers Nvidia's public endorsement and preferred access to its sought-after AI chips, putting the company in a position to use those benefits as leverage. Customers seeking the status must now enroll in Fleet Intelligence, according to people familiar with the new requirements. Fleet Management was introduced last December primarily as a data centre management tool. It gathers information about NVIDIA hardware deployed across participating companies' infrastructure, including the locations of individual accelerators. That information could give Nvidia a better picture of whether its chips are operating after they leave the supply chain.

The system is, however, a universal tracking mandate. Participation has remained voluntary outside the NCP requirement, and Nvidia does not automatically receive location data from every customer using the software. The company has also not clarified whether existing Cloud Partners will have to enroll to retain their designation or whether the requirement will eventually extend beyond Southeast Asia.

Notably, Nvidia has been developing a way to locate AI for some time now. Last December, we reported that the company had developed software-based GPU tracking, using GPU telemetry and network latency measurements to estimate where processors are operating. Nvidia described the software as a customer-installed agent for monitoring hardware health, integrity and inventory.

The technology has an obvious use beyond data maintenance. The U.S. restricts Chinese companies from purchasing advanced AI accelerators without a license, yet authorities believe billions of dollars worth of the hardware have reached Chinese buyers through third-party networks, with Southeast Asia becoming an important transit point for those transactions.

A C4ADS investigation has previously identified several routes used to move restricted processors into China, including purchases through research initiatives, shell companies and shipments routed through other Southeast Asian countries. The nonprofit examined transactions between 2022 and 2025 and identified $13.4 million worth of Nvidia A100, H100, and AD102-series GPUs moving through regional trade routes. Its report also linked $4.6 billion in hardware purchases to Singapore-based Megaspeed International.

Megaspeed has become a particularly awkward case for Nvidia, with the company being under U.S. and Singaporean investigation for more than a year over suspected chip diversion. Last week, Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang defended his dealings with the firm, describing them as “completely normal,” adding, “we’ve studied all their deployments of our GPUs and counted every one.” Megaspeed denied any wrongdoing. In April, Megaspeed’s Malysisan data centre landlord terminated its contract, reallocating 68.4 megawatts of capacity to another cloud provider

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

Moreover, Megaspeed is not the only example of how difficult it can be to police the movement of AI hardware. Earlier this year, U.S. prosecutors accused people associated with Super Micro of helping divert around $2.5 billion worth of Nvidia-powered servers to China through Southeast Asian intermediaries. The alleged scheme involved falsified paperwork and dummy server shells, with genuine hardware shipped onward after inspections. More recently, the company has faced additional scrutiny over its export compliance after inspections. Nvidia has also reportedly removed half of the Asian companies on its pre-approved buyer list and told cloud providers to keep at least 51% of their computing services with customers outside China.

The biggest 'will they, won't they' in gaming right now involves Arc Raiders and whether it will finally add a PvE mode. All seemed promising after a very successful PvE test during the start of Frozen Trial, but news has since petered out, with Embark saying it needs a bit more time to figure everything out.

I thought this meant Embark needed time to get all its ducks in a row and figure out the logistics of a permanent PvE mode, but it turns out that adding one isn't actually that hard.

"We could just frankly add PvE,” Embark's CEO Patrick Söderlund says in an interview with The Game Business. “It’s not a difficult thing to do. But we didn’t want to obstruct or fiddle too much with the core loop of the game where the PvP aspect of a PvPvE game does add, we believe, adequate tension."

The biggest issue concerning a PvE mode is what it means for the game itself. The whole point of Arc Raiders being PvPvE is so players suffer even more tension as you never know when you're going to be jumped by an arc or other player. PvE obviously removes one of those threats, and the worry is that "by removing one substantial component, you’re fiddling with the core emotional loop." Something that Embark has previously decided "was not the right thing to do".

"We’ve now since tried it and realised that it’s fine," Söderlund adds. "Maybe people can choose what they like. But that was our fear all along. People may have been wondering, 'why the hell aren’t they just doing what some players were looking for?' We were looking at the holistic picture, which we have to do. And to protect the creative integrity of what we have created."

I understand the draw to maintaining the creative vision, but unfortunately there are compromises to be made in a live service game. After releasing a game to players a certain amount of control gets handed away also, because in order to encourage players to come back and keep playing your game you need to give them what they want.

"We’re going through that data right now," executive producer Aleksander Grøndal says. "What we did see, which was really interesting, is that there was a huge amount of interest in it, and there were a lot of players who tried it out, and almost 50% of the rounds that were played during the test were on the PvE server. It created a moment.

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

"Some of the interesting takeaways are that some people who said, ‘Hey, I want PvE,’ changed their mind. They didn’t want it because it was lacking some of the values that the product had. And then you had players who absolutely loved it.

So the reality of the situation is clear: "Now it’s for us to figure out how we can cater to the specific needs of the players. But of course, not at the expense of the integrity of the experience itself, where you water it down and become a game of nothing.”

Cheap online services routed web traffic through a White House-linked network, researchers say

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Public services sell access to other people’s internet connections. Layer3 Intel says its tests repeatedly sent traffic through White House-linked addresses.

Threat-intelligence firm Layer3 Intel says public online services repeatedly sent its web traffic through a White House-linked network. The company recorded 236 observations between Oct. 5 and Nov. 3 across six internet addresses and 28 services, raising concern about outsiders using government-linked internet connections.

Layer3 identifies the network as hosting White House connectivity. AS2650 is listed under the name EOP-ASN in ARIN's registration record. The registration does not identify the device or person behind any particular connection.

Residential proxy services sell customers access to other devices' internet connections. As Layer3 describes the arrangement, traffic sent through a proxy appears to come from the device's network, rather than the customer buying access.

In its Nov. 4 report, Layer3 said it observed exits on 26 of the 30 days in its monitoring window. Its pipeline encountered the network while requesting global rotating access through residential proxy services, the company said, rather than deliberately selecting AS2650.

Layer3 said anyone could obtain the same type of access by paying a public residential proxy service a few dollars.

Layer3 said it cross-checked the addresses against public IP context pages from proxy-detection firms Spur and Synthient. The company said those pages also identified the addresses as residential proxy exits, but it has not disclosed the six addresses in its published finding.

Those figures describe recorded exits, public addresses and proxy services. They do not establish how many devices were involved or that each observation was an attack.

ARIN registers AS2650 to the Executive Office of the President. Layer3 reports 236 exit observations during Oct. 5–Nov. 3, 2026.

Services, public addresses and exit observations measure different parts of Layer3's finding.

The raw logs were not independently examined. Public-address counts are not device counts; internal access is not demonstrated.

Source-bound schematic using reported counts and independent ARIN registration. Dots represent 28 services and six public addresses; connectors show conceptual stages, not measured per-service mappings, traffic volumes or internal topology. No derived arithmetic or per-day chart reconstruction.

The raw logs were not independently examined. Public-address counts are not device counts; internal access is not demonstrated.

Layer3 said about half the activity occurred between 5 p.m. and midnight Eastern, with almost none in the early morning. It interpreted that pattern as suggesting a person's device running proxy software, rather than a public guest Wi-Fi network.

The company described an infected device with a proxy software development kit (SDK) as its explanation for the pattern, while acknowledging that its data shows neither the device nor the person behind the exits.

The distinction also matters for the security implications. Layer3 warned that poorly secured proxy software can, in some circumstances, expose internal systems. Its warning about this network was conditional: an SDK would need to have the kind of flaw documented in earlier proxy research for that route into internal systems to exist.

The published observations do not demonstrate access to internal White House systems. CryptoSlate has not reviewed the raw logs.

Layer3 said it had backed up its activity logs and was willing to share them with relevant agencies through its contact page.

RuntimeWire reported that founder Sergiy Usatyuk told it Layer3 would not disclose the specific addresses because of the sensitivity of the findings and had not notified anyone.

KrebsOnSecurity reported on Nov. 4 that the White House did not immediately respond to its request for comment.

Fabregas reacts to Como’s historic 9-1 Champions League victory: ‘Once in a lifetime’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Cesc Fabregas has spoken in-depth about Como’s recent 9-1 thrashing of Lens in the Champions League, says that he is disappointed with the goal that his team conceded, but says that he is ‘very happy’ for teenage goalscorer Matteo Papaccioli, who scored with his first chance on his first competitive appearance.

Como became the first ever Italian club to score nine goals in the Champions League and recorded the highest winning margin in an away fixture in the competition’s history when they beat Lens in a 10-goal thriller on Wednesday night.

It was a magnificent display that brought goals from seven different scorers in Tasos Douvikas (x3), Nico Paz, Lucas Da Cunha, Marc-Oliver Kempf, Mattia Liberali, Martin Baturina and the teenage Papaccioli.

As things stand after the fourth round of Champions League fixtures, Como sit in fourth place in the league phase table with nine points, behind only PSG, Real Madrid and Arsenal. Fabregas’s side have also inflicted 4-1 Champions League defeats on both RB Leipzig and Manchester United this season: the club’s first ever appearance in the competition.

“It’s true, we wrote ourselves into the history books,” Fabregas reflected in an interview with La Gazzetta dello Sport. “It was an unforgettable evening for our fans and for the club. Everything went our way, but we have to carry on like this because we have another important game on Sunday.”

Up next for the high-flying Como, who are currently fourth in the Serie A table as well, is a tip to San Siro to face reigning champions Inter.

“It’s right that we enjoy this moment because the group deserves it, but football moves at a very fast pace. I’ve already told the boys that we have to archive the Lens game and only think about Inter,” said Fabregas.

“A very tough game lies ahead of us at the home of the reigning Italian champions. It will be a tough challenge from a physical and mental point of view. We don’t have much time but we have to prepare as best as we can.

“The goal is to go to Milan and show our true identity. It will be our ninth game between the two international breaks. We have to try and finish well and then turn the page again.”

Returning to the midweek thrashing of Lens, Fabregas insisted that this is ‘only the beginning’ of the journey for Como in Europe.

“Nobody could have expected a 9-1 scoreline, a result that doesn’t normally exist in football. Something like that happens once in a lifetime. I’m proud of my boys. The numbers are excellent, but we’re only at the beginning of our journey in the Champions League. We’ve played four games.

“We had a good game against Lens, even if the first five minutes of the second half were a bit superficial. But, we did well in the defensive phase as well as in attack, even if the goal we conceded made me angry. Some things changed at the back when we made three substitutions, but it was my former teammate Aguilar who scored a great goal.”

Fabregas was asked what pleased him most about the victory over Lens.

He replied: “A result like that always catches your eye, but I like to see the team in training, always trying to give everything. We stuck to the game plan by constantly getting forwards and always going about it with our idea of how to play. That’s what made me most happy, because we played with full respect for the opponent until the final minute; pressing and trying to play our football.”

It has been an impressive bounce-back for Como after tasting their first Champions League defeat away against Feyenoord in October. They have since beaten Manchester United and Lens and scored 13 goals in the process. Fabregas was asked what has changed since that night in Rotterdam.

“Certainly not the attitude, which has always stayed the same,” he replied. “A lot of the time, we only look towards the result. We lost against Feyenoord because they scored three goals and were calmer than us in front of goal, that’s all.

“Against Lens, everything we did was positive and I felt that the players were excited. We definitely made some mistakes in the Netherlands, but we put the same level of aggression and attitude into it.”

Como are now in a position where qualification for the round of 16 is becoming an increasing possibility. They still have big matches against PSG and Barcelona coming up, though.

“The standings are getting interesting now. The Champions League is a competition involving lots of great teams and our goal is to fight with all of them that come our way.

“When I watched the draw and saw that we would have to go and play against PSG and Barcelona in the final two weeks, I, like everyone, thought we would need to pick up as many points as possible beforehand. Nothing is impossible in football.

“PSG and Barca are at a different level to the rest, but we will still have to play them at the end of January. There are still lots of games left. I hope that by then we will have brought back as many of our attackers as possible, which is where we’re suffering right now.”

Fabregas was also asked for his thoughts on 18-year-old Papaccioli, who scored the historic ninth goal for Como against Lens.

He said: “I’m very happy for Papa, we all are. I couldn’t help myself, we all went and joined him when he scored the goal for 9-1. It wasn’t a lack of respect, but an impromptu reaction after everything the young lad had experienced.

“Papa started training with us when he was 16, then he tore his cruciate and was out for nine months. He came back not long ago and now he has the opportunity to score goals. He’s a young player from our academy, he’s a humble and ambitious lad. He works hard and he deserves all of this.

“I thought on Wednesday that the conditions were right for him to make his debut. It doesn’t often happen that your debut comes in the Champions League, let alone scoring with your first chance. It was a night he’ll never forget.”

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

Comcast’s Sky Receives Security Threat After RTE Evacuates Staff

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Comcast‘s UK network Sky has received a “security threat,” hours after Irish broadcaster RTÉ took the decision to evacuate staff amid a series of alerts across Europe.

“Sky was one of a number of media organizations that received a security threat,” said a Sky spokesperson.

“We have carried out ‌a security assessment and reported the matter to the relevant authorities. The safety and security of our colleagues is our primary concern, and we are working closely with authorities to ensure our people and operations remain secure. It would not be appropriate to comment further at this time.” Related Stories Casting James D'Arcy, Jonathan Pryce & Lena Olin Board 'The Girl With The Dragon Tattoo' As Sky Series Casts The Vanger Family News Sky-ITV: UK Competition Bodies Given Greenlight To Investigate $2.2B Deal By Culture Secretary

The statement was read out during a Sky News broadcast this afternoon. Watch on Deadline

Earlier today, RTÉ decided to evacuate staff after receiving a similar security threat and other Irish newsrooms along with Bulgaria’s Nova TV also reported an incident appearing to follow the same pattern.

RTÉ Deputy Director General Adrian Lynch told the channel’s News at One program that he and Director General Kevin Bakhurst had been informed of a threat, and the decision was made to “put staff safety first” and evacuated the broadcaster’s Dublin office.

“We took this threat very seriously and I have to say it’s extremely sinister, and it’s also very sinister that this happened to a number of media organizations simultaneously,” added Lynch.

RTE has said its email threat came from the same group that has been issuing bomb threats to passenger airlines this week, with the sender naming themselves “Mikhail Andropov.” The identity of the sender has not yet been verified.

No explosives have been found on the planes that were targeted.
